+    v.external_id = write.external_id;
+    v.location = write.location;
+    v.cpu_count = write.cpu_count;
+    v.ram_mb = write.ram_mb;
+    v.gpu_type = write.gpu_type;
+    v.display_name = write.display_name;
+    v.last_rpc_time = write.last_rpc_time;
+    v.credit_total = write.credit_total;
+    return v;
+}
+
+} // namespace
+
+messaging::list_hosts_response
+host_service::list_hosts(const messaging::list_hosts_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_hosts_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.hosts = repo_.read_latest(ctx_, request.offset, request.limit);
+    response.total = repo_.get_total_host_count(ctx_);
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::get_host_response host_service::get_host(const messaging::get_host_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_host_response response;
+    auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key);
+    if (found.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.host = std::move(found.front());
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::get_many_hosts_response
+host_service::get_many_hosts(const messaging::get_many_hosts_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_many_hosts_response response;
+    // One entry per requested key, in the order asked for, so the reply is
+    // positional and a caller reads absence from an empty entry rather than
+    // from a missing one.
+    response.entries.reserve(request.keys.size());
+    for (const auto& k : request.keys) {
+        messaging::host_lookup entry;
+        entry.key = k;
+        auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, k);
+        if (!found.empty())
+            entry.host = std::move(found.front());
+        response.entries.push_back(std::move(entry));
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::put_host_response host_service::put_host(const messaging::put_host_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_host_response response;
+    domain::host value;
+    response.result = prepare_change(request.change, request.intent, value);
+    if (response.result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok)
+        return response;
+    repo_.write(ctx_, value, request.change.precondition);
+    auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+    if (!written.empty())
+        response.host = std::move(written.front());
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::put_many_hosts_response
+host_service::put_many_hosts(const messaging::put_many_hosts_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_many_hosts_response response;
+    std::vector<domain::host> batch;
+    batch.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes) {
+        domain::host value;
+        const auto result = prepare_change(change, request.intent, value);
+        if (result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok) {
+            // Nothing has been written: the whole set is checked before any
+            // of it lands, so a refused element refuses the batch.
+            response.result = result;
+            return response;
+        }
+        batch.push_back(std::move(value));
+    }
+    // One statement, so the set lands together. The store checks each row's
+    // claim inside that statement, which is what makes the check above and the
+    // write one decision rather than two.
+    std::vector<ores::utility::domain::precondition> claims;
+    claims.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes)
+        claims.push_back(change.precondition);
+    repo_.write(ctx_, batch, claims);
+    response.hosts.reserve(batch.size());
+    for (const auto& value : batch) {
+        auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+        response.hosts.push_back(written.empty() ? value : std::move(written.front()));
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_host_response
+host_service::delete_host(const messaging::delete_host_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_host_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_not_exist) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "A removal cannot require that a row is absent.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    std::optional<std::uint32_t> expected;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_match_version) {
+        if (!request.removal.precondition.version) {
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_incomplete";
+            response.result.message = "A versioned removal must state the version it expects.";
+            return response;
+        }
+        expected = request.removal.precondition.version;
+    }
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.removal.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    switch (repo_.remove(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), expected)) {
+        case repository::host_repository::remove_status::removed:
+            break;
+        case repository::host_repository::remove_status::missing:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+            response.result.code = "not_found";
+            break;
+        case repository::host_repository::remove_status::conflicting:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+            response.result.code = "version_conflict";
+            break;
+        case repository::host_repository::remove_status::unsupported:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+            response.result.message = "This resource keeps no version to match.";
+            break;
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_many_hosts_response
+host_service::delete_many_hosts(const messaging::delete_many_hosts_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_many_hosts_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        if (removal.precondition.kind != precondition_kind::any) {
+            // The store removes a set in one statement, which carries no
+            // per-row version. Refusing is the only answer that keeps the
+            // batch atomic: serving it as a sequence of single removals would
+            // leave a partial batch behind as soon as one row had moved on.
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "batch_removal_is_unconditional";
+            response.result.message =
+                "A batch removal is unconditional; remove the rows one at a time "
+                "to state a version.";
+            return response;
+        }
+    }
+    if (request.removals.empty())
+        return response;
+    // A removal names its row by the key a caller holds, and the repository
+    // takes the storage key, so the two are joined once here rather than at
+    // each column's conversion. A name that matches no row is skipped: the
+    // batch reports what it removed, and a row that is already gone is not a
+    // failure.
+    std::vector<domain::host> resolved;
+    resolved.reserve(request.removals.size());
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, removal.key);
+        if (!named.empty())
+            resolved.push_back(std::move(named.front()));
+    }
+    std::vector<std::string> id_keys;
+    id_keys.reserve(resolved.size());
+    for (const auto& row : resolved)
+        id_keys.push_back(boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    repo_.remove(ctx_, id_keys);
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::list_host_versions_response
+host_service::list_host_versions(const messaging::list_host_versions_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_host_versions_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.filter) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "filter_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "Filtering is not served for this resource yet.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    // The versions of the row the caller's key names. The repository reads by
+    // the storage key, so the declared key is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto all = repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    // The store reads versions newest first, and the order a caller gets when
+    // it states none is key order, which for a version key is oldest first.
+    std::reverse(all.begin(), all.end());
+    response.total = all.size();
+    const auto begin = std::min<std::size_t>(request.offset, all.size());
+    const auto end = std::min<std::size_t>(begin + request.limit, all.size());
+    response.versions.assign(std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + begin),
+                             std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + end));
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::get_host_version_response
+host_service::get_host_version(const messaging::get_host_version_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_host_version_response response;
+    // The version key nests the entity's own key, which is the declared one.
+    // The repository reads by the storage key, so it is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key.host);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto found = repo_.read_at_version(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), request.key.version);
+    if (!found) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.version = std::move(*found);
+    return response;
+}
+
+ores::utility::domain::result
+host_service::prepare_change(const messaging::host_change& change,
+                             const ores::utility::domain::change_intent& intent,
+                             domain::host& out) {
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    ores::utility::domain::result result;
+    out = to_domain(change.write);
+    const auto current = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(out));
+    switch (change.precondition.kind) {
+        case precondition_kind::must_not_exist:
+            if (!current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "already_exists";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::must_match_version:
+            if (current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+                result.code = "not_found";
+                return result;
+            }
+            // The protocol states the version as a uint32 and the row carries it
+            // as an int, so the comparison states the conversion.
+            if (!change.precondition.version ||
+                static_cast<std::uint32_t>(current.front().version) !=
+                    *change.precondition.version) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "version_conflict";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::any:
+            break;
+    }
+    // The version is the repository's to state, from the claim: it is the one
+    // thing the store's arbiter reads, and stating it in two places is how the
+    // two come to disagree.
+    stamp(out,
+          ctx_,
+          intent.reason_code.empty() ?
+              std::string(ores::service::messaging::change_reasons::new_record) :
+              intent.reason_code);
+    out.change_commentary = intent.commentary;
+    return result;
+}
+

-std::vector<domain::host> host_service::get_host_history(const std::string& id) {
-    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for compute host. " << "id: " << id;
-    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, id);
+std::vector<domain::host> host_service::get_host_history(const std::string& key) {
+    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for compute host. key: " << key;
+    // The caller holds the key the model declares and this reads by the
+    // storage key, so the two are joined here exactly as they are for any
+    // other read. Without this step a provider looks the versions up under a
+    // value the storage key never holds, and reports an entity that has a
+    // history as having none.
+    messaging::host_key k;
+    k.external_id = key;
+    // A delete here closes the transaction-time window and leaves every version
+    // in place, so resolving through a latest read would lose the history at
+    // exactly the moment it is wanted. This takes the newest row carrying the
+    // declared key whether or not it is still current, which for a record that
+    // still exists is the same row the latest read would have returned.
+    const auto found = repo_.read_any_by_external_id(ctx_, k.external_id);
+    if (found.empty())
+        return {};
+    const auto& row = found.front();
+    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
 }

 result_service::result_service(context ctx)
     : ctx_(std::move(ctx)) {}
+namespace {
+
+/**
+ * @brief The current row a key names, or an empty vector when there is none.
+ *
+ * A key record carries each column with the column's own type, and the
+ * repository takes the text form every one of its key parameters shares, so
+ * the conversion lives here rather than at every call site.
+ *
+ * The key record carries the key the model declares, which is the one a caller
+ * holds. When that is not the storage key the row is found by it and the
+ * repository's storage-key read is not used at all.
+ */
+std::vector<domain::result> read_one(repository::result_repository& repo,
+                                     const ores::database::context& ctx,
+                                     const messaging::result_key& key) {
+    return repo.read_latest_by_modified_by(ctx, key.modified_by);
+}
+
+/**
+ * @brief The key a domain object states, so a written row can be read back.
+ *
+ * A create states its own key in the write record, so the key of the row a
+ * write produced is the one the object carries.
+ */
+messaging::result_key key_from(const domain::result& v) {
+    messaging::result_key key;
+    key.modified_by = v.modified_by;
+    return key;
+}
+
+/**
+ * @brief Builds the domain object a write record states.
+ *
+ * The record carries the user-owned fields and nothing else: tenancy,
+ * provenance, the version and the validity window are the service's and the
+ * database's to state, and are set after this conversion.
+ */
+domain::result to_domain(const messaging::result_write& write) {
+    domain::result v;
+    v.id = write.id;
+    v.workunit_id = write.workunit_id;
+    v.host_id = write.host_id;
+    v.pgmq_msg_id = write.pgmq_msg_id;
+    v.server_state = write.server_state;
+    v.outcome = write.outcome;
+    v.output_uri = write.output_uri;
+    v.error_message = write.error_message;
+    v.received_at = write.received_at;
+    return v;
+}
+

+messaging::get_result_response

+    if (found.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";

+messaging::get_many_results_response
+result_service::get_many_results(const messaging::get_many_results_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_many_results_response response;
+    // One entry per requested key, in the order asked for, so the reply is
+    // positional and a caller reads absence from an empty entry rather than
+    // from a missing one.
+    response.entries.reserve(request.keys.size());
+    for (const auto& k : request.keys) {
+        messaging::result_lookup entry;
+        entry.key = k;
+        auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, k);
+        if (!found.empty())
+            entry.result = std::move(found.front());

+messaging::put_result_response
+result_service::put_result(const messaging::put_result_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_result_response response;
+    domain::result value;
+    response.result = prepare_change(request.change, request.intent, value);
+    if (response.result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok)
+        return response;
+    repo_.write(ctx_, value, request.change.precondition);
+    auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+    if (!written.empty())
+        response.result = std::move(written.front());
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::put_many_results_response
+result_service::put_many_results(const messaging::put_many_results_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_many_results_response response;
+    std::vector<domain::result> batch;
+    batch.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes) {
+        domain::result value;
+        const auto result = prepare_change(change, request.intent, value);
+        if (result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok) {
+            // Nothing has been written: the whole set is checked before any
+            // of it lands, so a refused element refuses the batch.
+            response.result = result;
+            return response;
+        }
+        batch.push_back(std::move(value));
+    }
+    // One statement, so the set lands together. The store checks each row's
+    // claim inside that statement, which is what makes the check above and the
+    // write one decision rather than two.
+    std::vector<ores::utility::domain::precondition> claims;
+    claims.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes)
+        claims.push_back(change.precondition);
+    repo_.write(ctx_, batch, claims);
+    response.results.reserve(batch.size());
+    for (const auto& value : batch) {
+        auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+        response.results.push_back(written.empty() ? value : std::move(written.front()));
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_result_response
+result_service::delete_result(const messaging::delete_result_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_result_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_not_exist) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "A removal cannot require that a row is absent.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    std::optional<std::uint32_t> expected;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_match_version) {
+        if (!request.removal.precondition.version) {
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_incomplete";
+            response.result.message = "A versioned removal must state the version it expects.";
+            return response;
+        }
+        expected = request.removal.precondition.version;
+    }
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.removal.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    switch (repo_.remove(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), expected)) {
+        case repository::result_repository::remove_status::removed:
+            break;
+        case repository::result_repository::remove_status::missing:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+            response.result.code = "not_found";
+            break;
+        case repository::result_repository::remove_status::conflicting:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+            response.result.code = "version_conflict";
+            break;
+        case repository::result_repository::remove_status::unsupported:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+            response.result.message = "This resource keeps no version to match.";
+            break;
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_many_results_response
+result_service::delete_many_results(const messaging::delete_many_results_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_many_results_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        if (removal.precondition.kind != precondition_kind::any) {
+            // The store removes a set in one statement, which carries no
+            // per-row version. Refusing is the only answer that keeps the
+            // batch atomic: serving it as a sequence of single removals would
+            // leave a partial batch behind as soon as one row had moved on.
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "batch_removal_is_unconditional";
+            response.result.message =
+                "A batch removal is unconditional; remove the rows one at a time "
+                "to state a version.";
+            return response;
+        }
+    }
+    if (request.removals.empty())
+        return response;
+    // A removal names its row by the key a caller holds, and the repository
+    // takes the storage key, so the two are joined once here rather than at
+    // each column's conversion. A name that matches no row is skipped: the
+    // batch reports what it removed, and a row that is already gone is not a
+    // failure.
+    std::vector<domain::result> resolved;
+    resolved.reserve(request.removals.size());
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, removal.key);
+        if (!named.empty())
+            resolved.push_back(std::move(named.front()));
+    }
+    std::vector<std::string> id_keys;
+    id_keys.reserve(resolved.size());
+    for (const auto& row : resolved)
+        id_keys.push_back(boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    repo_.remove(ctx_, id_keys);
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::list_result_versions_response
+result_service::list_result_versions(const messaging::list_result_versions_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_result_versions_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.filter) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "filter_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "Filtering is not served for this resource yet.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    // The versions of the row the caller's key names. The repository reads by
+    // the storage key, so the declared key is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto all = repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    // The store reads versions newest first, and the order a caller gets when
+    // it states none is key order, which for a version key is oldest first.
+    std::reverse(all.begin(), all.end());
+    response.total = all.size();
+    const auto begin = std::min<std::size_t>(request.offset, all.size());
+    const auto end = std::min<std::size_t>(begin + request.limit, all.size());
+    response.versions.assign(std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + begin),
+                             std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + end));
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::get_result_version_response
+result_service::get_result_version(const messaging::get_result_version_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_result_version_response response;
+    // The version key nests the entity's own key, which is the declared one.
+    // The repository reads by the storage key, so it is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key.result);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto found = repo_.read_at_version(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), request.key.version);
+    if (!found) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.version = std::move(*found);
+    return response;
+}
+
+ores::utility::domain::result
+result_service::prepare_change(const messaging::result_change& change,
+                               const ores::utility::domain::change_intent& intent,
+                               domain::result& out) {
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    ores::utility::domain::result result;
+    out = to_domain(change.write);
+    const auto current = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(out));
+    switch (change.precondition.kind) {
+        case precondition_kind::must_not_exist:
+            if (!current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "already_exists";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::must_match_version:
+            if (current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+                result.code = "not_found";
+                return result;
+            }
+            // The protocol states the version as a uint32 and the row carries it
+            // as an int, so the comparison states the conversion.
+            if (!change.precondition.version ||
+                static_cast<std::uint32_t>(current.front().version) !=
+                    *change.precondition.version) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "version_conflict";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::any:
+            break;
+    }
+    // The version is the repository's to state, from the claim: it is the one
+    // thing the store's arbiter reads, and stating it in two places is how the
+    // two come to disagree.
+    stamp(out,
+          ctx_,
+          intent.reason_code.empty() ?
+              std::string(ores::service::messaging::change_reasons::new_record) :
+              intent.reason_code);
+    out.change_commentary = intent.commentary;
+    return result;
+}

-std::vector<domain::result> result_service::get_result_history(const std::string& id) {
-    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for compute result. " << "id: " << id;
-    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, id);
+std::vector<domain::result> result_service::get_result_history(const std::string& key) {
+    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for compute result. key: " << key;
+    // The caller holds the key the model declares and this reads by the
+    // storage key, so the two are joined here exactly as they are for any
+    // other read. Without this step a provider looks the versions up under a
+    // value the storage key never holds, and reports an entity that has a
+    // history as having none.
+    messaging::result_key k;
+    k.modified_by = key;
+    // A delete here closes the transaction-time window and leaves every version
+    // in place, so resolving through a latest read would lose the history at
+    // exactly the moment it is wanted. This takes the newest row carrying the
+    // declared key whether or not it is still current, which for a record that
+    // still exists is the same row the latest read would have returned.
+    const auto found = repo_.read_any_by_modified_by(ctx_, k.modified_by);
+    if (found.empty())
+        return {};
+    const auto& row = found.front();
+    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
 }

 workunit_service::workunit_service(context ctx)
     : ctx_(std::move(ctx)) {}
+namespace {
+
+/**
+ * @brief The current row a key names, or an empty vector when there is none.
+ *
+ * A key record carries each column with the column's own type, and the
+ * repository takes the text form every one of its key parameters shares, so
+ * the conversion lives here rather than at every call site.
+ *
+ * The key record carries the key the model declares, which is the one a caller
+ * holds. When that is not the storage key the row is found by it and the
+ * repository's storage-key read is not used at all.
+ */
+std::vector<domain::workunit> read_one(repository::workunit_repository& repo,
+                                       const ores::database::context& ctx,
+                                       const messaging::workunit_key& key) {
+    return repo.read_latest_by_input_uri(ctx, key.input_uri);
+}
+
+/**
+ * @brief The key a domain object states, so a written row can be read back.
+ *
+ * A create states its own key in the write record, so the key of the row a
+ * write produced is the one the object carries.
+ */
+messaging::workunit_key key_from(const domain::workunit& v) {
+    messaging::workunit_key key;
+    key.input_uri = v.input_uri;
+    return key;
+}
+
+/**
+ * @brief Builds the domain object a write record states.
+ *
+ * The record carries the user-owned fields and nothing else: tenancy,
+ * provenance, the version and the validity window are the service's and the
+ * database's to state, and are set after this conversion.
+ */
+domain::workunit to_domain(const messaging::workunit_write& write) {
+    domain::workunit v;
+    v.id = write.id;
+    v.batch_id = write.batch_id;
+    v.app_version_id = write.app_version_id;
+    v.input_uri = write.input_uri;
+    v.config_uri = write.config_uri;
+    v.priority = write.priority;
+    v.target_redundancy = write.target_redundancy;
+    v.canonical_result_id = write.canonical_result_id;
+    return v;
+}

+messaging::list_workunits_response
+workunit_service::list_workunits(const messaging::list_workunits_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_workunits_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.filter) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "filter_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "Filtering is not served for this resource yet.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.workunits = repo_.read_latest(ctx_, request.offset, request.limit);
+    response.total = repo_.get_total_workunit_count(ctx_);
+    return response;

+messaging::list_by_batch_id_workunits_response workunit_service::list_by_batch_id_workunits(
+    const messaging::list_by_batch_id_workunits_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_by_batch_id_workunits_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.filter) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "filter_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "Filtering is not served for this resource yet.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.scope == ores::utility::domain::scope::subtree) {

+        return response;

+messaging::get_workunit_response
+workunit_service::get_workunit(const messaging::get_workunit_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_workunit_response response;
+    auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key);
+    if (found.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.workunit = std::move(found.front());
+    return response;
+}

+messaging::get_many_workunits_response
+workunit_service::get_many_workunits(const messaging::get_many_workunits_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_many_workunits_response response;
+    // One entry per requested key, in the order asked for, so the reply is
+    // positional and a caller reads absence from an empty entry rather than
+    // from a missing one.
+    response.entries.reserve(request.keys.size());
+    for (const auto& k : request.keys) {
+        messaging::workunit_lookup entry;
+        entry.key = k;
+        auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, k);
+        if (!found.empty())
+            entry.workunit = std::move(found.front());
+        response.entries.push_back(std::move(entry));
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::put_workunit_response
+workunit_service::put_workunit(const messaging::put_workunit_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_workunit_response response;
+    domain::workunit value;
+    response.result = prepare_change(request.change, request.intent, value);
+    if (response.result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok)
+        return response;
+    repo_.write(ctx_, value, request.change.precondition);
+    auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+    if (!written.empty())
+        response.workunit = std::move(written.front());
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::put_many_workunits_response
+workunit_service::put_many_workunits(const messaging::put_many_workunits_request& request) {
+    messaging::put_many_workunits_response response;
+    std::vector<domain::workunit> batch;
+    batch.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes) {
+        domain::workunit value;
+        const auto result = prepare_change(change, request.intent, value);
+        if (result.outcome != ores::utility::domain::outcome::ok) {
+            // Nothing has been written: the whole set is checked before any
+            // of it lands, so a refused element refuses the batch.
+            response.result = result;
+            return response;
+        }
+        batch.push_back(std::move(value));
+    }
+    // One statement, so the set lands together. The store checks each row's
+    // claim inside that statement, which is what makes the check above and the
+    // write one decision rather than two.
+    std::vector<ores::utility::domain::precondition> claims;
+    claims.reserve(request.changes.size());
+    for (const auto& change : request.changes)
+        claims.push_back(change.precondition);
+    repo_.write(ctx_, batch, claims);
+    response.workunits.reserve(batch.size());
+    for (const auto& value : batch) {
+        auto written = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(value));
+        response.workunits.push_back(written.empty() ? value : std::move(written.front()));
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_workunit_response
+workunit_service::delete_workunit(const messaging::delete_workunit_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_workunit_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_not_exist) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "A removal cannot require that a row is absent.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    std::optional<std::uint32_t> expected;
+    if (request.removal.precondition.kind == precondition_kind::must_match_version) {
+        if (!request.removal.precondition.version) {
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_incomplete";
+            response.result.message = "A versioned removal must state the version it expects.";
+            return response;
+        }
+        expected = request.removal.precondition.version;
+    }
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.removal.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    switch (repo_.remove(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), expected)) {
+        case repository::workunit_repository::remove_status::removed:
+            break;
+        case repository::workunit_repository::remove_status::missing:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+            response.result.code = "not_found";
+            break;
+        case repository::workunit_repository::remove_status::conflicting:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+            response.result.code = "version_conflict";
+            break;
+        case repository::workunit_repository::remove_status::unsupported:
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "precondition_not_supported";
+            response.result.message = "This resource keeps no version to match.";
+            break;
+    }
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::delete_many_workunits_response
+workunit_service::delete_many_workunits(const messaging::delete_many_workunits_request& request) {
+    messaging::delete_many_workunits_response response;
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        if (removal.precondition.kind != precondition_kind::any) {
+            // The store removes a set in one statement, which carries no
+            // per-row version. Refusing is the only answer that keeps the
+            // batch atomic: serving it as a sequence of single removals would
+            // leave a partial batch behind as soon as one row had moved on.
+            response.result.outcome = outcome::invalid;
+            response.result.code = "batch_removal_is_unconditional";
+            response.result.message =
+                "A batch removal is unconditional; remove the rows one at a time "
+                "to state a version.";
+            return response;
+        }
+    }
+    if (request.removals.empty())
+        return response;
+    // A removal names its row by the key a caller holds, and the repository
+    // takes the storage key, so the two are joined once here rather than at
+    // each column's conversion. A name that matches no row is skipped: the
+    // batch reports what it removed, and a row that is already gone is not a
+    // failure.
+    std::vector<domain::workunit> resolved;
+    resolved.reserve(request.removals.size());
+    for (const auto& removal : request.removals) {
+        auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, removal.key);
+        if (!named.empty())
+            resolved.push_back(std::move(named.front()));
+    }
+    std::vector<std::string> id_keys;
+    id_keys.reserve(resolved.size());
+    for (const auto& row : resolved)
+        id_keys.push_back(boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    repo_.remove(ctx_, id_keys);
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::list_workunit_versions_response
+workunit_service::list_workunit_versions(const messaging::list_workunit_versions_request& request) {
+    messaging::list_workunit_versions_response response;
+    if (!request.order.field.empty() || request.order.descending) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "order_not_supported";
+        response.result.message =
+            "This store pages in key order and cannot order by a stated field.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    if (request.filter) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::invalid;
+        response.result.code = "filter_not_supported";
+        response.result.message = "Filtering is not served for this resource yet.";
+        return response;
+    }
+    // The versions of the row the caller's key names. The repository reads by
+    // the storage key, so the declared key is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto all = repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
+    // The store reads versions newest first, and the order a caller gets when
+    // it states none is key order, which for a version key is oldest first.
+    std::reverse(all.begin(), all.end());
+    response.total = all.size();
+    const auto begin = std::min<std::size_t>(request.offset, all.size());
+    const auto end = std::min<std::size_t>(begin + request.limit, all.size());
+    response.versions.assign(std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + begin),
+                             std::make_move_iterator(all.begin() + end));
+    return response;
+}
+
+messaging::get_workunit_version_response
+workunit_service::get_workunit_version(const messaging::get_workunit_version_request& request) {
+    messaging::get_workunit_version_response response;
+    // The version key nests the entity's own key, which is the declared one.
+    // The repository reads by the storage key, so it is resolved once here.
+    const auto named = read_one(repo_, ctx_, request.key.workunit);
+    if (named.empty()) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    const auto& row = named.front();
+    auto found = repo_.read_at_version(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id), request.key.version);
+    if (!found) {
+        response.result.outcome = ores::utility::domain::outcome::missing;
+        response.result.code = "not_found";
+        return response;
+    }
+    response.version = std::move(*found);
+    return response;
+}
+
+ores::utility::domain::result
+workunit_service::prepare_change(const messaging::workunit_change& change,
+                                 const ores::utility::domain::change_intent& intent,
+                                 domain::workunit& out) {
+    using ores::utility::domain::outcome;
+    using ores::utility::domain::precondition_kind;
+    ores::utility::domain::result result;
+    out = to_domain(change.write);
+    const auto current = read_one(repo_, ctx_, key_from(out));
+    switch (change.precondition.kind) {
+        case precondition_kind::must_not_exist:
+            if (!current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "already_exists";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::must_match_version:
+            if (current.empty()) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::missing;
+                result.code = "not_found";
+                return result;
+            }
+            // The protocol states the version as a uint32 and the row carries it
+            // as an int, so the comparison states the conversion.
+            if (!change.precondition.version ||
+                static_cast<std::uint32_t>(current.front().version) !=
+                    *change.precondition.version) {
+                result.outcome = outcome::conflict;
+                result.code = "version_conflict";
+                return result;
+            }
+            break;
+        case precondition_kind::any:
+            break;
+    }
+    // The version is the repository's to state, from the claim: it is the one
+    // thing the store's arbiter reads, and stating it in two places is how the
+    // two come to disagree.
+    stamp(out,
+          ctx_,
+          intent.reason_code.empty() ?
+              std::string(ores::service::messaging::change_reasons::new_record) :
+              intent.reason_code);
+    out.change_commentary = intent.commentary;
+    return result;
+}

+std::optional<domain::workunit>
+workunit_service::get_workunit_by_input_uri(const std::string& input_uri) {
+    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting workunit by input_uri: " << input_uri;
+    messaging::workunit_key k;
+    k.input_uri = input_uri;
+    auto found = read_one(repo_, ctx_, k);
+    if (found.empty())
+        return std::nullopt;
+    return found.front();
+}

-std::vector<domain::workunit> workunit_service::get_workunit_history(const std::string& id) {
-    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for workunit. " << "id: " << id;
-    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, id);
+std::vector<domain::workunit> workunit_service::get_workunit_history(const std::string& key) {
+    BOOST_LOG_SEV(lg(), debug) << "Getting history for workunit. key: " << key;
+    // The caller holds the key the model declares and this reads by the
+    // storage key, so the two are joined here exactly as they are for any
+    // other read. Without this step a provider looks the versions up under a
+    // value the storage key never holds, and reports an entity that has a
+    // history as having none.
+    messaging::workunit_key k;
+    k.input_uri = key;
+    // A delete here closes the transaction-time window and leaves every version
+    // in place, so resolving through a latest read would lose the history at
+    // exactly the moment it is wanted. This takes the newest row carrying the
+    // declared key whether or not it is still current, which for a record that
+    // still exists is the same row the latest read would have returned.
+    const auto found = repo_.read_any_by_input_uri(ctx_, k.input_uri);
+    if (found.empty())
+        return {};
+    const auto& row = found.front();
+    return repo_.read_all(ctx_, boost::uuids::to_string(row.id));
 }

diff --git a/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_app_versions_create.sql b/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_app_versions_create.sql
index 5ac5fd35cb..c99eefab15 100644
--- a/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_app_versions_create.sql
+++ b/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_app_versions_create.sql
@@ -98,7 +98,17 @@ begin
     for update;
 
     if found then
-        if NEW.version != 0 and NEW.version != current_version then
+        -- The write states what it believes about the row, and the store is
+        -- what decides. Version zero means one thing: no current row exists.
+        -- So a create that collides with a live row is refused here, for every
+        -- client, rather than by a check each client has to remember.
+        if NEW.version = 0 then
+            if not ores_utility_version_replace_allowed_fn() then
+                raise exception
+                    'Row already exists: a create cannot replace it. State the version you read to replace the row, or ask for a version replace.'
+                    using errcode = '23505';
+            end if;
+        elsif NEW.version != current_version then
             raise exception 'Version conflict: expected version %, but current version is %',
                 NEW.version, current_version
                 using errcode = 'P0002';

-    entity_name text := 'ores.compute.app_version';

     changed_tenant_id text;

-        changed_id := OLD.id;
+        change_action := 'deleted';
+        changed_wrapper_version := OLD.wrapper_version;
+        changed_version := OLD.version;
         changed_tenant_id := OLD.tenant_id::text;
+    elsif TG_OP = 'UPDATE' then
+        -- A versioned table's update is the internal close of the current
+        -- row; the insert that follows it carries the change. Announcing
+        -- both would report one change twice, so the close announces
+        -- nothing.
+        return null;
     else
-        changed_id := NEW.id;
+        -- The first version of a row is a create; every later one is an
+        -- update, because the row it replaces was already there.
+        if NEW.version <= 1 then
+            change_action := 'created';
+        else
+            change_action := 'updated';
+        end if;
+        changed_version := NEW.version;
+        changed_wrapper_version := NEW.wrapper_version;
         changed_tenant_id := NEW.tenant_id::text;
     end if;
 
+    changed_key := jsonb_build_object('wrapper_version', changed_wrapper_version);
+
     notification_payload := jsonb_build_object(

-        'timestamp', ores_utility_iso8601_timestamp_fn(change_timestamp),
-        'entity_ids', jsonb_build_array(changed_id),
+        'event_id', gen_random_uuid()::text,
+        'entity', 'ores.compute.app_version',
+        'key', changed_key::text,
+        'action', change_action,
+        'version', changed_version,
+        'occurred_at', ores_utility_iso8601_timestamp_fn(clock_timestamp()),
+        'correlation_id', nullif(current_setting('ores.request.correlation_id', true), ''),
         'tenant_id', changed_tenant_id
     );

diff --git a/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_results_notify_trigger_create.sql b/projects/ores.sql/create/compute/compute_results_notify_trigger_create.sql

@@ -27,23 +27,46 @@ create or replace function ores_compute_results_notify_fn()
 returns trigger as $$
 declare
     notification_payload jsonb;
-    entity_name text := 'ores.compute.result';
-    change_timestamp timestamptz := NOW();
+    change_action text;
+    changed_version integer := 0;
     changed_id uuid;
+    changed_key jsonb;
     changed_tenant_id text;
 begin
     if TG_OP = 'DELETE' then
+        change_action := 'deleted';
         changed_id := OLD.id;
+        changed_version := OLD.version;
         changed_tenant_id := OLD.tenant_id::text;
+    elsif TG_OP = 'UPDATE' then
+        -- A versioned table's update is the internal close of the current
+        -- row; the insert that follows it carries the change. Announcing
+        -- both would report one change twice, so the close announces
+        -- nothing.
+        return null;
     else
+        -- The first version of a row is a create; every later one is an
+        -- update, because the row it replaces was already there.
+        if NEW.version <= 1 then
+            change_action := 'created';
+        else
+            change_action := 'updated';
+        end if;
+        changed_version := NEW.version;
         changed_id := NEW.id;
         changed_tenant_id := NEW.tenant_id::text;
     end if;
 
+    changed_key := jsonb_build_object('id', changed_id);
+
     notification_payload := jsonb_build_object(
-        'entity', entity_name,
-        'timestamp', ores_utility_iso8601_timestamp_fn(change_timestamp),
-        'entity_ids', jsonb_build_array(changed_id),
+        'event_id', gen_random_uuid()::text,
+        'entity', 'ores.compute.result',
+        'key', changed_key::text,
+        'action', change_action,
+        'version', changed_version,
+        'occurred_at', ores_utility_iso8601_timestamp_fn(clock_timestamp()),
+        'correlation_id', nullif(current_setting('ores.request.correlation_id', true), ''),
         'tenant_id', changed_tenant_id
     );

+        -- The write states what it believes about the row, and the store is
+        -- what decides. Version zero means one thing: no current row exists.
+        -- So a create that collides with a live row is refused here, for every
+        -- client, rather than by a check each client has to remember.
+        if NEW.version = 0 then
+            if not ores_utility_version_replace_allowed_fn() then
+                raise exception
+                    'Row already exists: a create cannot replace it. State the version you read to replace the row, or ask for a version replace.'
+                    using errcode = '23505';
+            end if;
+        elsif NEW.version != current_version then
             raise exception 'Version conflict: expected version %, but current version is %',
                 NEW.version, current_version
                 using errcode = 'P0002';
